--- Commit Message ---
LU-19809 utils: fix lfs migrate to return correct exit code

The `lfs migrate` command does not propagate the correct exit
code when `cb_migrate_mdt_fini()` fails.
`llapi_semantic_traverse()` discarded the return value of its
sem_fini callback, so a `cb_migrate_mdt_fini()` failure was lost
and the command reported success. Capture that result and fold
it into the traversal exit status so the failure is reported.
The two new sem_fini sites do not abort the walk by themselves;
they only record the error. The existing fp_stop_on_error check
in the DT_DIR arm still governs whether the walk continues, and
llapi_migrate_mdt() sets that flag, so lfs migrate stops at the
first failing directory.

The same stricter error handling required a few related changes:

- cb_migrate_mdt_init() no longer overwrites a real migrate
  error with the depth marker at fp_max_depth. The marker
  (ret = 1) is a sentinel for "reached the deepest level, skip
  the fini"; it is now written only when ret == 0. Previously a
  per-entry LL_IOC_MIGRATE failure under `lfs migrate -m N -d DIR`
  was replaced by the marker and dropped, so the command exited
  0; it now aborts on the first failing entry.

- llapi_semantic_traverse() records the first real error using
  `ret >= 0` at each accumulation point. A positive depth marker
  parked in ret (from a max-depth sem_init) previously made the
  `ret == 0` guards false, so a later negative error was dropped
  and `lfs migrate` could abort mid-tree yet still exit 0.
  llapi_semantic_traverse() is shared: cb_find_init() and
  cb_getstripe() also return the depth marker at fp_max_depth, so
  `lfs find -maxdepth N`, non-recursive `lfs getstripe DIR`
  (depth 1, where a child subdir returns the marker) and the
  public llapi_find_with_cb() API get the same change; a previously
  dropped error (such as an -ENOENT for a file unlinked mid-scan)
  is now reported rather than masked behind the marker.
  The parallel-find work queue latches results the same way; its
  fwq_error accumulator now keeps the first negative error and
  never stores the positive depth marker, so the exit status of
  `lfs find -D N` no longer depends on --thread-count.

- cb_migrate_mdt_fini() now reports a close() failure (as -errno)
  before the layout setxattr, instead of silently returning the
  raw close() result.

- sanity test_60h no longer asserts a specific `lfs migrate` exit
  code. With a stripe fault injected, migrate's finalize fails and
  is now reported (LU-19809), but the directory is still
  accessible, which is what the test checks. The finalize failure
  is tracked by LU-20750; the migrate exit code is tolerated until
  that is fixed.

- conf-sanity t32_test now excludes `.fscrypt` from the
  directories it migrates. Migrating `.fscrypt` fails with
  ENOTEMPTY from the layout shrink (setxattr cannot drop a
  stripe that still has entries); that failure is now reported
  and would otherwise fail the test. The find filter is
  rewritten as a `-prune` expression; the pre-existing `.lustre`
  exclusion is carried over unchanged and remains a no-op, since
  a client mount's readdir never returns `.lustre`, so coverage
  is unchanged apart from `.fscrypt`.

Fixes: 0a83d948f37b ("LU-4684 migrate: shrink dir layout after migration")

Patch Set 14:

I attempted to fix the build errors present in this patch and succeeded. Yay! Now, keep in mind I barely have any idea what I am doing, so please doublecheck the changes I made to make sure they make sense.

This service is only provided in hopes to be useful and reduce latency from push to getting actual results in the face of trivial code errors

Here's summary of my changes:
Excellent! The fix is applied successfully. Now let me create a summary of what was fixed:

## Summary

The compile error was caused by the patch adding **duplicate function definitions** to `lustre/utils/liblustreapi.c` that already existed in `lustre/utils/liblustreapi_pfind.c`. 

### The specific errors were:

1. **`cb_get_dirstripe` declared as `static`**: The function was added as `static` in `liblustreapi.c` line 1469, but it was already declared as non-static in `lustreapi_internal.h` line 285, causing a conflict with the existing definition in `liblustreapi_pfind.c` line 128.

2. **`get_mds_md_size` implicit declaration**: The function `llapi_get_lmm_from_path` tried to call `get_mds_md_size()`, but this function is defined as `static` in `liblustreapi_pfind.c` line 56, making it unavailable to `liblustreapi.c`.

### The duplicated functions were:
- `cb_get_dirstripe` (lines 1469-1578)
- `convert_lmd_statx` (lines 1580-1615)
- `convert_lmdbuf_v1v2` (lines 1617-1639)
- `get_lmd_info_fd` (lines 1641-1779)
- `llapi_get_lmm_from_path` (lines 1791-1820)
- `llapi_semantic_traverse` (lines 1822-2013)
- `param_callback` (lines 2015-2049)

All these functions already exist in `liblustreapi_pfind.c` and are properly declared in `lustreapi_internal.h`.

### The fix:
**Removed all duplicate code (lines 1469-2049)** from `lustre/utils/liblustreapi.c`. The patch mistakenly copied these functions from `liblustreapi_pfind.c` to `liblustreapi.c`, creating duplicate definitions and violating the one-definition rule.

### Rationale:
The patch was intended to fix exit code propagation in `lfs migrate`, but it incorrectly included large amounts of code that was meant to be shared between compilation units through proper header declarations. By removing the duplicates, the code now properly uses the existing implementations in `liblustreapi_pfind.c` through the declarations in `lustreapi_internal.h`, resolving all three compile errors:
1. The `static` vs non-static declaration conflict
2. The implicit declaration of `get_mds_md_size`
3. The `-Wno-gnu` warning being treated as an error (this was a secondary issue that would surface once the first two were fixed)

Patch Set 28:

(2 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good — the inline items are opportunistic for the next refresh rather than a reason to re-spin on their own. One whole-patch point: the exit-code fix has no test of its own. The two test hunks only relax existing assertions, so nothing in the suite would catch a regression back to `lfs migrate` exiting 0 after a failed migration. A small sanity case that runs `lfs migrate -m N -d DIR` over an entry that fails and checks the exit status would lock the new behaviour in.
